# Changelog

The one changelog of the project, published as the Changelog page of the documentation site. Each entry is a dated heading followed by bullets that state what changed and where; the newest entry stands first. An entry of the release names the lane whose work it records, and an entry of the work before the release names its subject. The entries of the release 0.01 stand under its heading, and the entries of the work before it under the heading "Before the release".

## After the release 0.04

### 2026-11-14 — The refusal of a control defined by itself gives its reason alone

- A line of a backward block that defines a control by itself, `c = c`, is refused under [`definition-order`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#definition-order) with its reason, and the refusal names no marked spelling to write (AAS, 14 November 2026). The sentence of 13 November advised `c = c[>]` with `c[>]` listed in the header, and the header of such a block refuses a marked control under [`field-perch`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#field-perch).
- The sentence for a value-type field, `L = L`, is unchanged: it still names `L = L[>]` and `L[<] = …`.

### 2026-11-14 — A method's entry in a methods file writes no number

- A methods file no longer writes a number among the arguments of a method word. Every number that a method reads is a setting, declared in the methods file and valued in the settings file, as the bounds and the counts of a grid already were (AAS, 14 November 2026). An entry such as `!gauss_hermite(n_ψ=7, n_ξ=7)` is refused where it is bound, under the new rule [`method-setting-declared`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#method-setting-declared), at any depth of a tuple or of a word's own arguments. It is written `!gauss_hermite(n_ψ=n_ψ_nodes, n_ξ=n_ξ_nodes)`, with `n_ψ_nodes : ℕ` and `n_ξ_nodes : ℕ` declared in the methods file and the two counts given in the settings file.
- `stage.settings` therefore lists every number that a method or a grid reads, and two versions of a stage that differ only in such a number have the same methods and differ in their configuration.
- The grammar is unchanged: it reads the number, and the refusal is made where the entry is bound, as for a number in a grid entry. A quoted string in a method's entry remains admitted and is no setting.
- No stage file or methods file under `applications/` or `tutorials/` wrote the form.
- The rule revises the sentence of [Appendix D](bellman-calculus/APPX-D-KINDS-AND-SORTS.md) §4 on the value of a keyword argument. [Methods](bellman-calculus/06-Methods.md) §§4 and 6, Appendix E.4, Appendix G, the abstract grammar's requirements on a typing entry, [Methodization](Bellman-Sym/04-methodization.md) §§3.1, 4 and 7, the reference of the method types and the wiki's entries on [methods](wiki/methods.md) and [settings](wiki/settings.md) state it.

### 2026-11-13 — Eleven corrections where a stage writes a second value-type field

Each correction has a test, and no other test of the package changes its outcome. A value-type [field](wiki/field.md) is one that a header lists with the role word `!vlu`, as the value `V` is.

- The card of a [builder](wiki/builder.md), the text that `denote()` prints, gives under each output of an Aggregation the line that writes it. With the lines of a value block in another order than its header lists the outputs, `L = L[>]` before `V = 𝑢(c) + β*V[>]` under `(V, L)`, the card printed each rule under the other output. Where a block began with a local definition, the card printed that definition as the rule of the value. The kernel was right in both cases and is unchanged.
- A derivative line, `D_{m}V = 𝑑𝑢(c)`, is recorded on the value that its left side names. In a block that writes two value-type fields the card printed the line under both, and `value_carries_derivative` answered that the second field carried the derivative.
- `bellman.trellis.derivative_missing` reads the derivative of an arrival value through an arrival block that also writes another field, `Θ[<] = Θ` beside `V[<] = V`. Before, a second field in a terminal stage made it report the derivative readings of the stages before it as unmet.
- A value-type field that the feasible set of a policy line reads, as `L[>]` in `Γ_c(x : ℝ₊₊) := {c : ℝ₊₊ | x - c >= L[>]}`, is an input of the policy builder. Its signature lists the field, the route from the next period requires it, and `stage.diagnostics()` records no `unread-input` for the listing. Before, the signature listed the value alone, and the route into a period that supplies no such field was certified.
- The connection report decides a named set written with one condition that compares its variable with a bound, `Xa := {x : ℝ | x > 0}` against `Xk := {x : ℝ | x > -0.3}`, as it decides the intervals `(0, inf)` and `(-0.3, inf)`. It reads a bound that is a [derived parameter](wiki/parameters.md), `j := l - 0.25`, at its value. Where it leaves a comparison undecided, its sentence gives the reason, and it no longer names the variable of a condition as a bound.
- `compile_kernel()` compiles `max` and `min` on a line of a block, `L = max(L[>], b)`, to the greater and the lesser of the arguments, by the rule that writes them in the definition of a derived parameter ([Expression forms](Bellman-Sym/reference/forms.md) §3). Before, the kernel of the whole Aggregation was refused under [`kernel-form`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#kernel-form), the component of the value with it.
- In a stage whose label is chosen, the check behind [`branch-tuple-mismatch`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#branch-tuple-mismatch) takes as the value line the line whose tuple the policy maximizes. A second field that the label selects, `Θ = (Θ_w[>], Θ_r[>])[d]`, written after the value line made the stage be refused; the stage is now read in either order of the two lines.
- A line of a backward block whose two sides are one bare name, `L = L`, is refused under [`definition-order`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#definition-order), as the same line of a forward block is. The sentence names the marks that tell the two fields apart, `L = L[>]` and `L[<] = …`.
- A named set or a declared function that reads bare a field which the headers list with a mark alone, `Xm := {x : ℝ | x > L}` where a header lists `L[>]`, is refused under [`name-in-scope`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#name-in-scope), with the file, the line and the marked spelling. Before, the elaboration raised a Python `KeyError`.
- The refusal under [`shock-observed`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#shock-observed) of an arrival line with no expectation advises the expectation over every shock that the edge draws, `E_{ψ,ξ}(…)`. Before, it advised the first shock alone, and the line written as advised was refused again.
- Where a block of a methods file typed `!explicit` stands in place of a stage's block and writes no line for an output that the stage's header lists, the refusal under [`output-defined`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#output-defined) names the block of the methods file and says that its lines stand in place of the stage's. Before, it pointed at the block of the stage file, which writes the line.

### 2026-11-12 — The forward record of a period, and one period's forward map and its fixed point

- `forward_record(period, inter_connector, to=None)`, of `bellman.period`, answers the **forward record** of a [period](wiki/period.md): what each [forward builder](wiki/forward_builder.md) of the period, the builder that carries the measure of a population from one perch of a stage to the next, needs and what it gives, as plain data that refers to no stage. For each one, in the order the builders are applied, a `ForwardStep` names the state fields it reads from the measure that reaches it, each shock it draws with the law the stage file declares and the values of the parameters that law reads, each control with the states its policy is read at, the law of motion as the function `compile_kernel()` answers with its parameters and their values, and the state fields of the measure it gives. The record then holds the joins within the period, the fields at which a measure enters and leaves the period, and the joins of the inter-period mapping, an entry such as `{"a": "k"}`, to the period given as `to` or, with no `to`, to the period itself. `needs()` lists what a numerical step must be given: nodes and weights for each builder's shocks, a function for each control, and a value for each parameter that has none. `closed()` says whether the mapping joins the period to itself at every field at which a measure enters it.
- The record is read from elaborated stages and binds nothing. A stage with one forward builder is held: a stage that declares no arrival perch, which is entered at its decision perch, and a stage that ends a model. A builder with a sub-builder or a branch, or whose law of motion has no explicit function, is refused with a `ValueError` that names the stage and the builder, and a stage whose forward builders are not constructed is refused by the stage as before.
- `asva.forward`, a new module of the package `asva`, computes with the record and with what its caller supplies, and imports nothing of `bellman`. A measure is held as weighted atoms, `Atoms`: for each field `(stage key, field)` an array of points, and an array of masses. `carry` carries a measure between two places of a period, through each builder in turn: the measure is extended by the table of the builder's shocks, each control is read from its policy, and the law of motion is applied. `one_period_ahead` carries a measure through the period and along the inter-period mapping, with a survivors' share of mass and the measure of those who enter in place of the rest. `transition` gives the same map on a product grid by linear lottery, `stationary` its fixed point, the measure `mu` with `mu = s T mu + (1 - s) nu` for the survivors' share `s` and the entrants' measure `nu`, by a linear solve or by iteration, and by power iteration where every household survives, and it reports whether an iteration converged and how much the measure changes over one more period. A grid is finite, and the lottery places at an end node the mass the map carries beyond it; `transition` holds how much each node sends below and above the grid, and `stationary` reports the share of its measure that the ends hold in that way, since a measure that keeps its place only because of that return, all the mass at the top node of a map that moves every household up, is no stationary measure of the map. `tabulated` makes a policy from a table of one state.
- Two questions are thereby asked of a period that has been built and solved, with no entry in a trellis file: where a given population stands one period ahead, and what population the period's policy would generate if the same period followed without end. The second is the stationary population of the period, and it is the stationary population of a model only where that policy is the model's.
- Death and birth can be stated in a stage file with the language as it stands: a stage in which a household survives with a declared probability and is otherwise replaced by a newborn, written with a label drawn from `Categorical` and a selection by that label. A period that holds such a stage has, as the fixed point of its own forward map, the measure that the survivors' share and the entrants give when they are passed as arguments; the tests of the record compare the two.

### 2026-11-12 — A selection by one label carries a value NumPy cannot convert

- The text of a compiled kernel writes a selection by a label, `(θ, ξ_u)[j]`, through an inner function, `where_label`, in place of `np.where`. Every operand NumPy takes gives what `np.where` gave, at one label or at each point of an array of labels. Where `np.where` refuses an operand it cannot convert, as it refuses a value that JAX is tracing, and the key is one label, the function returns the component the label selects at the common shape of the operands, the shape `np.where` gives its result, so that a caller can trace such a kernel with the label fixed, map it over quadrature nodes or differentiate it; before, JAX was refused at `np.where`. A selection by an integer key is written with `np.where` as before, and Numba compiles it as before (`packages/bellman/tests/test_finite_indexed_data.py`).

### 2026-11-12 — Calibration refuses a faulty value at its own name before a definition computes with it

- `calibrate` judges each value against its own declared set before it evaluates a definition of a [derived parameter](wiki/parameters.md) that reads the value. With `ξ_u : ℝ₊ @ [0, ξ_u_sup)`, `ξ_u_sup := 1/p_zero`, `s := (1 - p_zero*ξ_u)/(1 - p_zero)` and `μ := log(s)`, a value of `ξ_u` at or above `1/p_zero` is refused at `ξ_u`, with the condition `ξ_u < ξ_u_sup` and the value of the bound. Before, the three definitions were evaluated first, and the calibration was refused under `derived-parameter` at `μ`, whose logarithm has no value there.
- The same holds where a second parameter's set reads a derived parameter computed from the faulty value, as `a_Art : (-inf, 0] @ (a_Nat, inf)` does where `a_Nat` is defined from `ξ_u`: the calibration is refused at `ξ_u` in whatever order the file declares the two. Before, the first of the two in the file was named.
- A derived value outside the set its definition declares is refused at that definition before another definition reads it: with `q : (0, 1) := p_zero*ξ_u` and `h := 1/(1 - q)`, a value of `q` of one is refused at `q`, and no longer at the division by zero in `h`.
- The derived parameter that a set reads is valued before the set is judged, wherever the file defines it. A parameter and a derived parameter that read each other, `x : ℝ @ (y, inf)` with `y := 2*x`, or through a further definition, `x : (0, z)` with `y := x + 1` and `z := 2*y`, are judged as before, after the definitions have their values.
- The values that the set of a value reads are judged with the value, and a set written by name, `Xu := [0, ξ_u_sup)` with `ξ_u : Xu`, is read through its definition. With a second parameter `ξ_mid : (ξ_u, ξ_u_sup)` and a definition that reads `ξ_mid` alone, a value of `ξ_u` at or above its bound is refused at `ξ_u`, and not at `ξ_mid`. A derived value outside the set its definition declares is refused at that definition, and not at a parameter whose set reads it.
- The values at which a stage calibrates are unchanged, and so are the values of the derived parameters. A calibration fails the same conditions as before; the change is in which of them the refusal names.

### 2026-11-12 — The agents' rule on parameters and settings

- `AGENTS.md` gains a rule of usage after "The model and its numerics are kept apart": "parameter" names an input of the mathematical model and "setting" an input of a numerical method, in documentation, comments, docstrings, names, commit messages and chat, also where another toolkit's documentation calls a method's input a parameter. The two words are defined in the concept wiki and in Appendix E.4 of the Bellman calculus; that the word "parameter" is kept for the quantities that define the economy was stated until now only on the wiki's page on functional forms, where it concerns the arguments of a form (CDC, 6 November 2026).

### 2026-11-12 — `min`, `max` and a definition by cases in a derived parameter, and one reading of an infinite endpoint

- The definition of a [derived parameter](wiki/parameters.md), a constant that a stage file or a methods file defines from its parameters by `:=`, may call `min` and `max`, each of one argument or more, beside `log` and `exp`, as in `min(R, Γ)`. Calibration evaluates every argument and takes the least or the greatest. A call of any other function is refused under [`derived-parameter`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#derived-parameter), or under [`not-constructed`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#not-constructed) where that call is the whole right side, as in `h := sqrt(R)`, and a call of one of the four where the stage defines a function of that name is refused under `derived-parameter` ([Equation parsing rules](Bellman-Sym/appendices/12-equation-parsing-rules.md) §2).
- A derived parameter may be defined by cases. The right side of its definition is then a conditional, `if test then a else b`, as in `h := if Γ < R then 1/(1 - Γ/R) else 0`, and the definition may declare a set before `:=` as any definition of a derived parameter may. The test compares two formulas by `<`, `<=`, `=`, `>=` or `>`, with `≤` and `≥` as second spellings of `<=` and `>=`, and each of the two branches `a` and `b` is a formula, a further conditional, `inf` or `-inf`. The value is the branch that the test chooses, and the other branch is not evaluated, so that a division it writes is not made. A conditional stands as the whole right side of a definition without arguments and as a branch of another conditional. Written inside a formula, in a function's body or in a line of a block, it is refused under [`syntax`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#syntax), and the refusal says where the form stands.
- A branch written `inf` or `-inf` gives that infinity as the derived parameter's value. Such a branch is admitted only where the [declared set](Bellman-Sym/reference/symbols-conventions.md#92-domains) of the definition, the set written before `:=` in which its value must lie, holds that infinity: a set of extended reals, the real numbers with an infinity adjoined, as in `h : ℝ_{+∞} := if Γ < R then 1/(1 - Γ/R) else inf`, or an interval closed at the infinite endpoint, such as `(0, inf]`. A definition that writes an infinite branch with no declared set, or with a set that by its form cannot hold the infinity, such as `(0, inf)` or `ℝ̄ @ (0, inf)`, is refused under `derived-parameter` when the file is read, and the refusal gives a set to write. At a calibration the value, finite or infinite, is judged against the declared set, and `stage.par` prints it.
- `if`, `then` and `else` are reserved words of the language. A file that writes one of them as a name, in a declaration, a definition, a function's argument, a block's header or a formula, is refused under `syntax`.
- An endpoint of a declared set that is the bare name, the name with no arithmetic, of a parameter or of a derived parameter valued at an infinity is read as that infinity: with `debt : ℝ₊ @ [0, h)`, the set of `debt` is `[0, ∞)` where `h` is `inf`. A bound that read a parameter declared on the extended reals at an infinite value, as `debt : [0, T)` reads `T : ℝ̄` at `T = inf`, was left open before, with the membership listed as required by `stage.obligations()`, and is decided now. `calibrate` also decides whether a number lies in a set of extended reals, which it left required before: a finite number lies in every such set, and an infinity in those that hold it, so that `inf` is refused for a parameter declared on `ℝ_{-∞}` ([Symbols and conventions](Bellman-Sym/reference/symbols-conventions.md) §9.2). In a calibration file an infinite value is written `.inf` or `-.inf`, which the package reads as a number; it reads `inf` there as a text, and the refusal of a declaration that carries the value, `T : ℝ̄ := inf`, advises `T: .inf`, where it advised `T: inf` before.
- A bound of a declared set, an endpoint or a second set written after `@`, may call `min` and `max` as it may call `log` and `exp`. `calibrate` decides the membership of a value in a set such as `β : ℝ₊₊ @ (0, min(R, 2))`, which it left required before, and `configure` refuses a [setting](wiki/settings.md)'s value outside such a set, which it bound before without a check.
- No arithmetic of infinities is defined. A definition that evaluates a name valued at an infinity, in a formula, in a test, in a call or as the bare name, has no value, and the calibration is refused at that definition. A bound that computes with such a name, as `2*T` does at `T = inf`, or with the written infinity, as `2*inf` does at every calibration, has no value either, and the calibration is refused at that bound; before, the membership was left required and the value was bound.
- A compiled kernel, the Python function that `compile_kernel()` answers for an operation of a stage ([Expression forms](Bellman-Sym/reference/forms.md) §3), writes the `min` and `max` of a derived parameter that it reads as `np.minimum` and `np.maximum`, and a definition by cases as a call of a function. Where the test is one truth value, that function evaluates the chosen branch alone. Where the test is an array, as when the kernel is called with an array of values of a parameter, the function takes at each entry the value of the branch that the test chooses there, and a branch neither raises nor warns at the entries where the test does not choose it. The same selection is made where the kernel's text is run with JAX's arrays and compiled by `jax.jit`: the compiler calls the function with placeholders in place of the numbers, so that the test has no one truth value, and both branches are evaluated.

### 2026-11-11 — A grid bound over a set whose endpoint is a parameter

- `configure` decides whether a grid's bound lies in the coordinate's declared set at the calibration that is bound, where the set reads a parameter: with `Xa := [a_Art, inf)` and a borrowing limit `a_Art` of zero, a savings grid from 0.001 to 20 configures. Earlier every such bound was refused under [`grid-bounds`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#grid-bounds) as "not decidably inside" the set, although the declared set of a setting was already read at the calibration; one value now receives one answer from both checks ([Methodization](Bellman-Sym/04-methodization.md) §3.2).
- A bound outside the set at the calibration is refused under `grid-bounds`, and the sentence names the parameter's value: "which lies outside the declared set Xa of a at a_Art = 0.0". The points of `!Points` and the nodes of a constructed grid are judged in the same way, so a declared point outside its set at the calibration, which was admitted undecided, is refused.
- Before a calibration is bound nothing decides such a membership, and a bound over such a set is refused as before, so `calibrate` comes before `configure` for a grid on a set that reads a parameter. A set that reads no parameter is decided without a calibration, as before.

## 0.04, the internal developer release

- The fourth internal release gathers the work on `main` since the tag `internal-dev-0.03`, whose dated entries stand below. Its principal parts are several sets in one type, written with `@`, with `calibrate` and `configure` deciding a set with conditions; a union and a difference of sets of numbers, and a declared set on a derived parameter, by which a condition on several parameters is stated; Appendix D of the calculus as the one account of the types, with the reference pages Rules and Types printed from tables of the package; the rule that, at a connection, the declared set of the outgoing field lies in that of the incoming field; and the homepage's type, spacing and layout.
- The release is the tag `internal-dev-0.04` of 12 November 2026 on `main`, at the commit that records it. On that commit, checked in a clean copy of the repository, `mkdocs build --strict` reported no warning and the check of the package's imports found no fault. The package's suite gave 1,231 tests passed, 24 skipped and 10 failed. The ten are those that failed at the release 0.03: they test the documentation's folders and navigation, the wiki's links, the branching examples (five tests), the trellis of the factory, a connector's qualified names and the applications' folder layout. The checks of the document properties and of the decision records reported 67 and 65 faults, against 69 and 65 at the release 0.03.

### 2026-11-12 — A union and a difference of sets, a condition on a derived parameter, and one account of the types

- A declared set may be a union or a difference of sets of numbers, written with `\/` and `\`, with `∪` and `∖` read as second spellings: `ρ : ℝ₊₊ \ {1}` declares a positive parameter other than one, and `ρ : (0,1) \/ (1, inf)` declares the same set. The product binds before the two signs and the arrow after them. The two forms are read in the set of a parameter, of a derived parameter and of a setting, and are refused elsewhere under [`readable-form`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#readable-form) ([Symbols and conventions](Bellman-Sym/reference/symbols-conventions.md) §9.2). `calibrate` and `configure` decide whether a value lies in a union or in a difference, and leave the membership required only where no member decides it.
- A derived parameter may carry a declared set on its definition, `q : (0,1) := (β*R)^(1/ρ)/R`, and `calibrate` then refuses a calibration at which the value of the formula lies outside the set. A condition on several parameters at once is stated in this way, as the set of a named formula of them. The table `stage.par` holds the set and the definition of such a name.
- Corrections. Two different perch words in one type are refused. Several sets with no number in common are refused, also where their endpoints are ratios of two integers, as in `(0,1) @ (1/2, 1/3)`; sets written alike count once. One interval that holds no number, `(2, 1)`, and a difference that holds no number, `(0,1) \ ℝ₊`, are refused. A line of a block that writes a parameter, a setting or a derived parameter is refused. The set of a parameter, of a setting or of a derived parameter that reads a field is refused, whether it reads the field directly, through a named set or through the law of a shock. `calibrate` refuses a calibration at which a bound has no value, and it values a bound that calls `log` or `exp`.
- Nine example stage files state in their declarations what they stated in words. The Epstein–Zin savings stage under `applications/epstein-zin-savings` declares `β : (0,1)` and the return patience factor at the certainty-equivalent return, `q : (0,1) := (β*exp((1-ρ)*(μ_Ψ + (1-γ)*σ_Ψ^2/2)))^(1/ρ)`, in place of a bound that held an expectation and that calibration never decided. The consumption-savings stage with i.i.d. income declares `γ : (1, inf)`, and seven stages exclude the value one of a curvature parameter with a difference of sets. The [perfect-foresight example](examples/consumption-perfect-foresight-crra/perfect_foresight.md) also writes the return impatience condition on the definition of its return patience factor, `q : (0,1) := (β*R)^(1/ρ)/R`, beside the bound of its discount factor.
- [Appendix D](bellman-calculus/APPX-D-KINDS-AND-SORTS.md) of the calculus is rewritten as the one account of the types: the words type, sort, kind and carrier; the admissible set of the parameters, to which each declaration and each typed definition adds one condition; the state signature and the operator signature of a stage; its forward and its backward model; and the Bellman operator as the arrival builder applied after the optimizing decision builder, at each version of a stage. Its paragraph on periods states the rule for a connection between stages that the entry of 11 November records. The other pages of the calculus, its catalogues and the wiki are brought into agreement with it.
- [Appendix C](Bellman-Sym/appendices/14-appendix-c-syntax-to-semantics.md) of Bellman-SYM states, rule by rule, what the package does, and lists in its §5.3 the rules that the package does not yet check. Two reference pages are printed from tables of the package: [Rules](Bellman-Sym/reference/rules.md), which gives for each rule the functions that implement and check it, the refusals it raises and one test, and [Types](Bellman-Sym/reference/types.md), which lists the objects that a file types, the forms of a set and the limits of the language.
- The package `bellman` is version 0.1.7. The lessons and the application notebooks that read a changed stage file, or that print the version, are executed again; their source cells are unchanged.

### 2026-11-11 — Compatible declared sets at a connection

- [Period Syntax](Bellman-Sym/06-period.md#2-connectors) §2 defines compatible declared sets at a connection: the set of the outgoing field is contained in the set of the incoming field, and the two sets need not be equal, since a declared set states where a field's values may lie and does not claim that every member is reached. [Trellis Syntax](Bellman-Sym/07-trellis.md#2-inter-period-connectors) §2 points to the definition. The check the package makes is unchanged.
- [Stage composition](bellman-calculus/07-stage-composition.md) §1 states the same rule in the terms of [Appendix D](bellman-calculus/APPX-D-KINDS-AND-SORTS.md), whose remarks record that a connection uses domain refinement, the continuation field's domain contained in the arrival field's, and not equality of domains.

### 2026-11-11 — Homepage type, spacing and layout

- The [homepage](index.md) sets its text in five sizes, stated for a window 1440 px wide: 96 px for the headline, 56 px for section headings, 24 px for titles and statements, 16 px for text, links and buttons, and 12 px for labels in capitals. Its distances are taken from eight steps between 4 px and 96 px, and every section stands on one grid of twelve columns. The sizes are stated at the head of `docs/_stylesheets/home.css`.
- The opening section shows the two triangles as one drawing at the left; the headline, the standfirst and the two buttons start on the centre line. The column of the lower triangle runs to its top corner, the top bar meets the column's side, and the orange block is the column's lowest block. The standfirst reads "A declarative, modular and machine verifiable language system for dynamic economics."
- The three tiles of the second section are Bellman language, Semantic ontology and AI interface, each with a new description.
- Applications shows Housing, Pensions and Buffer-stock saving as three tabs in a row above the model, which takes the full width of the page. The sentence that described each model and its link to the example page are removed; the links to all examples and to the Workbench guide remain. The left and right arrow keys move between the tabs, as the up and down keys do.
- The diagram of a model, which stood at the upper left of the Bellman semantics pane, stands in the middle of the pane.
- GitHub stands alone at the right-hand end of the header, after a menu that ends with AI. The footer has a Contribute link beside About us, Docs and GitHub.
- In a window of 767 px or less the drawing stands above the headline, and the tabs take a second and a third row where the window is too narrow for one.

### 2026-11-11 — Several sets in one type

- A declaration may write several sets in one type, with `@` between them, and the declared name then lies in each of them: `β : (0,1) @ (0, R^(ρ-1))` declares a discount factor below one and below `R^(ρ-1)`. A bound is an interval, whose endpoints may name parameters and whose unbounded side is `-inf` or `inf`, as in `θ : ℤ @ [1, inf)`. At most one of the sets is written otherwise than as an interval, the sets may stand in any order, and the form is read on the declaration of one name on its own line ([Symbols and conventions](Bellman-Sym/reference/symbols-conventions.md) §9.2).
- The earlier spelling of a bound, a condition after a comma as in `β : (0,1), < R^(ρ-1)`, is retired. A file that writes it is refused under [`syntax`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#syntax), and the refusal gives the sets to write.
- `calibrate` decides whether a value lies in a set with conditions: the several sets of a type, and a named set written with a bar such as `Xb := {x : (0,1) | x < R}`. It refuses a value that breaks a bound and names the bound and the values it read, and `configure` does the same for a setting. Earlier every value of such a set was accepted, a value outside its base included. Where a set reads another parameter, as a bound or as an endpoint, a faulty value is refused at its own parameter, and not at the parameter whose set reads it, in whatever order the file declares the two. A bound that reads a random variable or a derived parameter is not decided and remains a stated requirement.
- The parameters line of a view and `stage.par` print a declared set as its base followed by one interval for each bound, `β : (0, 1) @ (0, R^(ρ - 1))`. The set does not depend on the order of the sets written or on how their endpoints are grouped into intervals, and a declaration that writes the printed text declares the same set. The stage sheet prints a named set with a bar as its definition writes it.
- The [perfect-foresight example](examples/consumption-perfect-foresight-crra/perfect_foresight.md) states the return impatience condition as the upper bound of its discount factor, beside the finite human wealth condition on its growth factor, and calibration refuses a calibration that breaks either, whichever methods file is bound. Its notebook is executed again.
- The [smooth-ambiguity example](examples/smooth-ambiguity/smooth_ambiguity_savings.md) declares `η : (1,inf) @ [γ, inf)`. The bound was strict, which excluded the ambiguity-neutral calibration `η = γ` that the stage's own checks use. The Epstein–Zin stage, one stage of the test corpus and twelve fixtures of the tests of Bellman Workbench and of the comparator are rewritten in the new spelling with their sets unchanged.

## 0.03, the internal developer release

- The third internal release gathers the work on `main` since the tag `internal-dev-0.02`, whose dated entries stand below. Its principal parts are Bellman Workbench with its desktop application and its guide; the trellis, the period file and the recipe, with parameters shared within a period and connections between stages; the Matsya client and its service; finite index sets, named integer domains, further continuous shock distributions and terminal continuation values in the language; and the methods file as an addition beside the stage file, with method blocks and marginal blocks, grids, representation families, explicit solutions and derived parameters.
- The release is the tag `internal-dev-0.03` of 10 November 2026 on `main`, at the commit that records it. On that commit, checked in a clean copy of the repository, `mkdocs build --strict` reported no warning and the check of the package's imports found no fault. The package's suite gave 1,175 tests passed, 25 skipped and 10 failed. The ten, which also failed before the day's entries, test the documentation's folders and navigation, the wiki's links, the branching examples (five tests), the trellis of the factory, a connector's qualified names and the applications' folder layout. The checks of the document properties and of the decision records reported 69 and 65 faults, the numbers they held before the day's entries.

### 2026-11-10 — The Workbench guide

- The [Workbench guide](workbench/index.md) gives task instructions for Bellman Workbench in nine pages, under a section of their own in the navigation: a quickstart on the supplied portfolio model, building a model with Matsya, opening and editing a project, inspecting a model, editing equations and inputs, creating and connecting stages, managing recipes and variants, and sharing a project and finding the cause of a problem.
- [Install](overview/00-install.md) and [Bellman Workbench](overview/07-workbench.md) point to the guide. The overview page's account of the Matsya column is replaced by a pointer to [Build a model with Matsya](workbench/01-matsya.md), and the supplied example of the install page is `applications/cons-port`.
- The navigation lists the example page of the perfect-foresight consumption model, [Perfect Foresight (CRRA)](examples/consumption-perfect-foresight-crra/perfect_foresight.md), under Examples.

### 2026-11-10 — Grids, function representations and explicit solutions

- A methods file types a [grid](wiki/grid.md) with `!Cartesian`, `!Points` or `!Fibred`. `!Points(nodes=P)` takes the points of `P`, a setting declared on a fixed finite product of points or a set defined by its members, in the declared order of the place's coordinates; `!Points` alone is the empty grid. `!Fibred(base=!Points(nodes=B), fibre=!Cartesian(c=(Γ_c, n_c)))` pairs each state of the base with `n_c` evenly spaced points of the feasible interval `Γ_c` at that state, endpoints included; it is admitted where the domain of the place is the product of those states and the control, as at a `!vfi` builder. Each constructor may end with `require=` and a predicate word, `!Empty`, `!Cartesian`, `!NonCartesian` or `!UniformCartesian`, or a tuple of them, which states a condition on the resulting grid; an unknown or contradictory word is refused where it is written ([Method types](Bellman-Sym/reference/methods.md); [Methods](bellman-calculus/06-Methods.md#5-the-grid-as-the-discretized-domain) §5).
- `configure` refuses a grid that fails a requirement its bound values decide, under [`grid-requirement`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#grid-requirement). Once the settings are bound, `kernels()` of a builder answers under `grids` the realized grid of each place within it that types a grid, with its constructor, its coordinates and its finite points, repeated points removed. Where the points or a required property cannot be established, as for an omitted factor, an open feasible interval or a `fibre` of two controls, whose joint sampling is unsupported, the entry is the refusal that says why, and no default is supplied. `same_grid` of `bellman.stage.grids` compares two realized grids on the same coordinates exactly, with no tolerance ([A builder](Bellman-Sym/API/stage/04-builder.md) §5).
- `!Linear`, `!pchip` and the closed form, written `!closed_form`, are representation families registered with the package. `register`, `unregister` and `families` of `bellman.stage.representations` add, remove and list families. A `RepresentationFamily` states its typed arguments, the data sources it accepts, its derivatives, its stored data and whether it reads a grid, and supplies no evaluator. A family registered after the package is imported may be named in a methods file, and a duplicate word or the word of another method is refused. A stage keeps the families it was bound with, so that a later registration or removal changes only later bindings.
- The record of each representation in `stage.methods` names its data source under `source`. The source is selected from all the methods files the stage holds, taken together, in a fixed order: an authored table `evaluate(knots, values, x)`, then the policy pairs that `!egm` selects at the closing Evaluation of its builder, then a complete authored expression, such as the lines of an `!explicit` builder, and otherwise the family's own nodes on its grid. `!Linear` and `!pchip` accept a table, the pairs or their own nodes; the closed form accepts an expression and refuses a grid. A source the family does not accept is refused under [`representation-source`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#representation-source), and no other source is chosen in its place; at the closing Evaluation of an `!egm` builder, a family that reads the pairs refuses a grid of its own. A grid beside an authored table holds exactly the table's knots: `configure` compares the two supports with no sorting and no tolerance, the two signed zeros being one point, and a support not yet known leaves the obligation `table-grid-support` in `stage.obligations()` ([Methods and places](Bellman-Sym/API/stage/07-methods.md)).
- `!explicit` types a policy builder or a backward value builder whose solution is known in closed form. Its block writes one line `output = expression` for each output, which may read the states and controls of its output perch, the parameters, the derived parameters and the names that earlier lines define. The lines replace the maximization at the operative level, `stage.declared` keeps it, and the compiled kernel of each line evaluates its output. A line that reads a value input or its derivative, states a relation, applies `argmax`, `solve` or `upper_env`, or reads a field at another perch is refused under [`explicit-solution`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#explicit-solution), and the same block typed `!egm` or `!time_iteration` is refused with the advice to type `!explicit`. Unless a representation is written at the builder's Evaluation, the Evaluation receives `!closed_form`, which `stage.methods` and the signature show with the entry it comes from, such as `generated from policy.method`; removing `!explicit` removes it ([Methodization](Bellman-Sym/04-methodization.md)).
- A stage file or a methods [addition](wiki/addition.md) defines a derived parameter with `:=` from declared parameters, numbers and earlier derived parameters, using arithmetic, powers, `log` and `exp`, such as `h := 1/(1 - Γ/R)`. The calibration supplies no value for it. `calibrate`, and `methodize` on a calibrated stage, evaluate the definitions before the checks of the distributions and their supports, so that a distribution whose scale is a negative derived value is refused under [`distribution-domain`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#distribution-domain). A cycle among the definitions, a name that is not a parameter and a call other than `log` and `exp` are refused at elaboration. A calibration that gives a derived parameter a value, or at which a definition has no finite real value, is refused at the definition under [`derived-parameter`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#derived-parameter). `stage.par` and `denote()` show each definition and, once the stage is calibrated, its value; the stored calibration keeps the declared parameters alone ([Parameters and settings](Bellman-Sym/API/stage/06-par-and-settings.md) §1.1).
- The new application `consumption-perfect-foresight-crra`, the perfect-foresight consumption model with utility of constant relative risk aversion, is solved in two ways from one stage file, which defines the return patience factor `q`, human wealth `h` and the natural borrowing limit `L` as derived parameters, with one calibration file ([Perfect foresight consumption with CRRA utility](examples/consumption-perfect-foresight-crra/perfect_foresight.md)). Its methods file `methods.bl` defines the marginal propensity to consume `κ := 1 - q` and types the policy builder and both value builders `!explicit`, with the closed-form consumption function and values; the replacing selection `methods.egm.bl` solves the stage by the endogenous grid method on a grid of savings, with `!Linear` at the policy builder's Evaluation. The application's module compares the two methods and evaluates the value functions with scalar Linear and PCHIP evaluators, which refuse a query outside the interval of their nodes unless the comparison of policies explicitly extends the Linear evaluator along its first and last segments, and each use of that extension is reported.
- In the buffer-stock application, the log-mean of the positive transitory draw is the derived parameter `μ_ξ := -log(1 - p_zero) - σ_ξ^2/2`, so that E[ξ] = 1 holds at every calibration, and a calibration that supplies a value for μ_ξ is refused under [`derived-parameter`](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md#derived-parameter). At `σ_ξ` = 0 the positive draw is the point mass at 1/(1 − p_zero) ([Buffer Stock Consumption Model](examples/buffer-stock/buffer_stock.md)).
- [Method types](Bellman-Sym/reference/methods.md) is generated from the records that the checks of a methods file read. Each method, each registered representation family and each grid constructor has one entry, which states its meaning, its declaration and the places where it may be written, its type parameters, its arguments and the meaning of their omission, the grids or data it accepts, an example, its admission and whether a numerical implementation is identified, and its links. An index of the places at which each method may stand, by operator, defined function, line and builder, precedes the method entries, and a table of the grid constructors and a table of the predicate words of `require=` precede the grid entries. An entry with no registered arguments says that unlisted keywords are retained for the numerical handler.

### 2026-11-09 — Method components and binding refusals

- A [grid entry](Bellman-Sym/reference/methods.md) without a method entry is validated on its own. Replacing a stage's methods checks retained setting values against the new declarations before changing the stage; a refused replacement preserves the earlier stage.
- Signature equality and hashing include the method and grid components beside inputs and outputs. Unpacking a signature still returns its inputs and outputs.
- `bind_stage` accepts an optional `on_refusal(error, verb)` conversion while retaining refusal strings by default. The Workbench uses the original exception to preserve its rule, position and correction, and groups the same binding refusal from the stage and trellis readings once.
- Target refusals separate their condition from the correction. The stage library and folder chooser list stage declarations without treating their methods files as additional stages.
- A bare name in a method argument must be a declared setting; quoted text remains a literal value. Finite sets containing `true` or `false` admit the corresponding Boolean settings and keep them distinct from numerical zero and one.
- The buffer-stock example retains its authored grid for evaluating the policy, with declared bounds and point count. Housing-renting's consumption additions declare each numerical option separately, preserving their existing values.
- Workbench removes outdated editor marks on editing and applies a diagnostic only to the file and text that were checked. Related source links select a range only when its checked version is known. Save, layout, desktop and Git failures retain their operation, explanation and retry action in the diagnostic details; a failed save preserves the edited text.

### 2026-11-07 — The methods file as an addition beside the stage file, on the how-to and reference pages

- [Methodization](Bellman-Sym/04-methodization.md) is written anew on the package as merged. A stage's methods stand in its methods file, `methods.bl` beside the stage file, a `.bl` file whose blocks name the stage's blocks by their ids and add typing entries to their brackets and lines to their bodies, and which the package reads when `load_stage`, `methodize` or a recipe names it. Its entries type the method and the grid of an operator, `policy.method : !egm` and `policy.grid : !Cartesian(a=(a_min, a_max, n_a))`, and name its handler, `policy.handler : "cons-port:egm"`; the paths of the places, the name of an expectation, `E_ψ_ξ` for `E_{ψ,ξ}`, the kinds of place and the refusals are stated; the settings a method reads are declared in the methods file with their sets and valued in the settings file; a solution method's lines stand in place of the stage file's maximization at the operative level while the declared level keeps it; the rules of the union and the replacing selection are given. The page quotes the buffer-stock methods file as it stands, prints `stage.methods`, the policy builder's signature and `stage.settings` from the package, and ends with the steps by which an economist writes the file for a model.
- [Method types](Bellman-Sym/reference/methods.md) reads its printed table with the entry each row writes, the keyword arguments the package reads and the perch its grid covers, and gains §3 on the grid: the kind `!Cartesian`, the four shapes of a factor, the first with an optional spacing word, and the default where a place writes no grid. [Expression forms](Bellman-Sym/reference/forms.md) §4 describes the blocks of a methods file in place of the block file, its row of `D` shows the first spelling of `D` as the package's printer writes it, and the prose of [Refusals](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md) names the rules under which an entry of a methods file is refused; the addresses `evaluate.value` and `evaluate.marginal`, the word `!marginals`, the key `block:`, the entry `on:` and the identity table are gone from every page but the historical example pages, which the examples index marks as the earlier YAML design and which keep their YAML listings; the Matsya pages still name the `methods.yml` file the service writes today.
- [Parameters and settings](Bellman-Sym/API/stage/06-par-and-settings.md) prints `stage.par` before and after calibration and `stage.settings` with the set each setting is declared in and the place that reads it, and [Methods and places](Bellman-Sym/API/stage/07-methods.md) prints the places with one Evaluation place for each stored object, `cntn_to_dcsn.evaluate[V]` and `cntn_to_dcsn.evaluate[D_{m_d}V]`, `stage.methods` with the word, the grid and the handler typed at each place, the printed signatures with their components, and the policy builder at its two levels. The other pages of the stage object are printed again on the consumption stage of the portfolio application as it stands, whose method lines are in its methods file, and the API index gains the rows of `typing_entries`, `write_typing_entry`, `card_sections` and `derivative_missing` and prints the rows of a line marked `!doc` on the portfolio stage.
- [Trellises](Bellman-Sym/07-trellis.md), [The Bellman factory](Bellman-Sym/08-bellman-factory.md), [Model directory layout](Bellman-Sym/reference/model-directory-layout.md), [Branching](Bellman-Sym/05-branching.md), [Constraints](Bellman-Sym/10-constraints.md), [Appendix C](Bellman-Sym/appendices/14-appendix-c-syntax-to-semantics.md), [Appendix D](Bellman-Sym/appendices/15-appendix-d-node-tag-and-field-indexing.md), the binding verbs and the stage factory of the API, the overview pages [Semantics](overview/05-semantics.md), [Workflow](overview/06-workflow.md), [Bellman Workbench](overview/07-workbench.md) and [Fields, evaluation and methods](overview/08-fields-evaluation-and-methods.md), and the wiki entry [specified stage](wiki/specified_stage.md) quote the applications' methods files as merged and describe one `methods.bl` for each stage folder, a replacing selection `methods.<variant>.bl` beside it, a second file named after the first that adds to its selection, and the recipe row `methods: {all: [stages/<key>/methods.bl]}`; the branching page types a method at a branching stage by the paths of its places, a line inside a branch named under its label; the Workbench page describes the Settings tab with the declared set beside each value and the Methods tab's choice written into the methods file.

- The Bellman calculus page [Methods](bellman-calculus/06-Methods.md) is written: an operator's signature carries its method and its grid as classifier components beside its inputs and outputs, each written as a typing of the operator's name, `policy.method : !egm` and `policy.grid : !Cartesian(a=(a_min, a_max, n_a))`; operators compose over their inputs and outputs alone and the labels of a composite are kept as a list; the method is a type over the kind of operator and takes keyword arguments; the grid is the discretized domain, a finite subset of the function's domain with its inclusion, over which a representation is a section; the settings a method reads are declared with their domains in the methods file and bound by `configure`; the handler is a binding beside the term and realizes the operator under Ρ; two erasures return the model, one forgetting the labels and one forgetting the methods file; a stage holds a declared and an operative level; nothing is differentiated. [Functional programming](bellman-calculus/APPX-G-FUNCTIONAL-PROGRAMMING.md) §7 states the two erasures and the section law with the identity label on every operator without an entry, and [Kinds and sorts](bellman-calculus/APPX-D-KINDS-AND-SORTS.md) §3 lists the grid as a sort of its own.

### 2026-11-01 — The grid as a tagged setting

- A representation names its basis as the method, `!Linear` for piecewise linear interpolation or `!pchip` for the shape-preserving cubic, and its grid under `settings`: the grid's kind is the tag on the value of the setting `grid`, and the grid's fields, `orders` and `bounds`, stand beside it, `settings: {grid: !Cartesian, orders: {m: n_m}, bounds: {m: [m_min, m_max]}}`. `!Cartesian` is a kind of grid and no longer a method; it admits the fields `orders` and `bounds`, both required, and, where written, `spacing` and `nodes`. The endogenous grid method names its exogenous grid of savings the same way under its own entry, in the states of the continuation perch, `orders: {a: n_a}`. A representation at the policy builder's Evaluation where `!egm` stands writes no grid, since the knots of its interpolant are the pairs the method stores, and a representation of an authored `evaluate(knots, values, x)` needs none ([Methodization](Bellman-Sym/04-methodization.md) §3; [Method types](Bellman-Sym/reference/methods.md)).
- Three forms are refused, each in one sentence naming the form to write or the reason: `!Cartesian` written as a method, `orders` and `bounds` written beside a basis or beside `!egm` without the setting `grid`, and a grid written at the policy builder's Evaluation under `!egm` ([Refusals](Bellman-Sym/reference/refusals.md)). A value of `grid` that names no kind of grid, the grid's fields written inside the tag, and the key `grid` written beside `method` are refused by the check of the options. The loader of a methods file, `load_methods` of `bellman.stage.sources`, ends a tag at a comma or a closing bracket inside a flow mapping, so that the form above written on one line is read as it is read when written over several lines.
- `configure` checks the fields of a grid by their kinds, those of the exogenous grid of `!egm` at the states of the continuation perch, and `stage.settings` gives in its column `grid` the kind of the grid of which a setting is a field. Every methods file of the applications and of the Workbench's test models writes the tagged form: the entries that wrote `!Cartesian` as their method write `!Linear`, the piecewise linear interpolation their routines carry out, and every `!egm` entry names its grid of savings; buffer-stock's settings file gains `n_a: 960`, `a_min: 0.001` and `a_max: 20.0`, and consumption-savings-iid and housing-owner gain a settings file for their grid of savings. The portfolio–consumption tool reads its grid of savings from the `!egm` entry, and the reference solution is reproduced in both orders.
